```
   296→// ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs extracts agent IDs from Task tool results in a transcript.
   297→// When a Task tool completes, the tool_result contains "agentId: <id>" in its content.
   298→// Returns a map of agentID -> toolUseID for all spawned agents.
   299→func ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs(transcript []TranscriptLine) map[string]string {
   300→	agentIDs := make(map[string]string)
   301→
   302→	for _, line := range transcript {
   303→		if line.Type != "user" {
   304→			continue
   305→		}
   306→
   307→		// Parse as array of content blocks (tool results)
   308→		var contentBlocks []struct {
   309→			Type      string          `json:"type"`
   310→			ToolUseID string          `json:"tool_use_id"`
   311→			Content   json.RawMessage `json:"content"`
   312→		}
   313→
   314→		var msg struct {
   315→			Content json.RawMessage `json:"content"`
   316→		}
   317→		if err := json.Unmarshal(line.Message, &msg); err != nil {
   318→			continue
   319→		}
   320→
   321→		if err := json.Unmarshal(msg.Content, &contentBlocks); err != nil {
   322→			continue
   323→		}
   324→
   325→		for _, block := range contentBlocks {
   326→			if block.Type != "tool_result" {
   327→				continue
   328→			}
   329→
   330→			// Content can be a string or array of text blocks
   331→			var textContent string
   332→
   333→			// Try as array of text blocks first
   334→			var textBlocks []struct {
   335→				Type string `json:"type"`
   336→				Text string `json:"text"`
   337→			}
   338→			if err := json.Unmarshal(block.Content, &textBlocks); err == nil {
   339→				var textContentSb361 strings.Builder
   340→				for _, tb := range textBlocks {
   341→					if tb.Type == "text" {
   342→						textContentSb361.WriteString(tb.Text + "\n")
   343→					}
   344→				}
   345→				textContent += textContentSb361.String()
   346→			} else {
   347→				// Try as plain string
   348→				var str string
   349→				if err := json.Unmarshal(block.Content, &str); err == nil {
   350→					textContent = str
   351→				}
   352→			}
   353→
   354→			// Look for agentId in the text
   355→			if agentID := extractAgentIDFromText(textContent); agentID != "" {
   356→				agentIDs[agentID] = block.ToolUseID
   357→			}
   358→		}
   359→	}
   360→
   361→	return agentIDs
   362→}
   363→
   364→// extractAgentIDFromText extracts an agent ID from text containing "agentId: <id>".
   365→func extractAgentIDFromText(text string) string {
   366→	const prefix = "agentId: "
   367→	idx := strings.Index(text, prefix)
   368→	if idx == -1 {
   369→		return ""
   370→	}
   371→
   372→	// Extract the ID (alphanumeric characters after the prefix)
   373→	start := idx + len(prefix)
   374→	end := start
   375→	for end < len(text) && (text[end] >= 'a' && text[end] <= 'z' ||
   376→		text[end] >= 'A' && text[end] <= 'Z' ||
   377→		text[end] >= '0' && text[end] <= '9') {
   378→		end++

   257→		relDeletedFiles = FilterAndNormalizePaths(changes.Deleted, repoRoot)
   258→	}

   950→// sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript checks if a session has new content
   951→// by examining the live transcript file. This is used when no shadow branch exists
   952→// (i.e., no Stop has happened yet) but the agent may have done work.
   953→//
   954→// Returns true if:
   955→//  1. The transcript has grown since the last condensation, AND
   956→//  2. The new transcript portion contains file modifications, AND
   957→//  3. At least one modified file overlaps with the currently staged files
   958→//
   959→// The overlap check ensures we don't add checkpoint trailers to commits that are
   960→// unrelated to the agent's recent changes.
   961→//
   962→// This handles the scenario where the agent commits mid-session before Stop.
   963→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript(repo *git.Repository, state *SessionState) (bool, error) {
   964→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   965→
   966→	// Need both transcript path and agent type to analyze
   967→	if state.TranscriptPath == "" || state.AgentType == "" {
   968→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "live transcript check: missing transcript path or agent type",
   969→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   970→			slog.String("transcript_path", state.TranscriptPath),
   971→			slog.String("agent_type", string(state.AgentType)),
   972→		)
   973→		return false, nil
   974→	}
   975→
   976→	// Get the agent for transcript analysis
   977→	ag, err := agent.GetByAgentType(state.AgentType)
   978→	if err != nil {
   979→		return false, nil //nolint:nilerr // Unknown agent type, fail gracefully
   980→	}
   981→
   982→	// Cast to TranscriptAnalyzer
   983→	analyzer, ok := ag.(agent.TranscriptAnalyzer)
   984→	if !ok {
   985→		return false, nil // Agent doesn't support transcript analysis
   986→	}
   987→
   988→	// Get current transcript position
   989→	currentPos, err := analyzer.GetTranscriptPosition(state.TranscriptPath)
   990→	if err != nil {
   991→		return false, nil //nolint:nilerr // Error reading transcript, fail gracefully
   992→	}
   993→
   994→	// Check if transcript has grown since last condensation
   995→	if currentPos <= state.CheckpointTranscriptStart {
   996→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "live transcript check: no new content",
   997→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   998→			slog.Int("current_pos", currentPos),
   999→			slog.Int("start_offset", state.CheckpointTranscriptStart),
  1000→		)
  1001→		return false, nil // No new content
  1002→	}
  1003→
  1004→	// Transcript has grown - check if there are file modifications in the new portion
  1005→	modifiedFiles, _, err := analyzer.ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(state.TranscriptPath, state.CheckpointTranscriptStart)
  1006→	if err != nil {
  1007→		return false, nil //nolint:nilerr // Error parsing transcript, fail gracefully
  1008→	}
  1009→
  1010→	// No file modifications means no new content to checkpoint
  1011→	if len(modifiedFiles) == 0 {
  1012→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "live transcript check: transcript grew but no file modifications",
  1013→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
  1014→		)
  1015→		return false, nil
  1016→	}
  1017→
  1018→	// Normalize modified files from absolute to repo-relative paths.
  1019→	// Transcript tool_use entries contain absolute paths (e.g., /Users/alex/project/src/main.go)
  1020→	// but getStagedFiles returns repo-relative paths (e.g., src/main.go).
  1021→	// Use state.WorktreePath (already resolved) to avoid an extra git subprocess.
  1022→	basePath := state.WorktreePath
  1023→	if basePath == "" {
  1024→		if wp, wpErr := GetWorktreePath(); wpErr == nil {
  1025→			basePath = wp
  1026→		}
  1027→	}
  1028→	if basePath != "" {
  1029→		normalized := make([]string, 0, len(modifiedFiles))

    20→// TestSessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript_NormalizesAbsolutePaths verifies
    21→// that sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript correctly normalizes absolute paths
    22→// from the transcript to repo-relative paths before comparing with staged files.
    23→//
    24→// Bug: ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset returns absolute paths (e.g., /tmp/repo/src/main.go)
    25→// but getStagedFiles returns repo-relative paths (e.g., src/main.go). The exact-string
    26→// comparison in hasOverlappingFiles never matches, causing "no content to link".
    27→func TestSessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript_NormalizesAbsolutePaths(t *testing.T) {
    28→	dir := setupGitRepo(t)
    29→	t.Chdir(dir)
    30→
    31→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(dir)
    32→	require.NoError(t, err)
    33→
    34→	s := &ManualCommitStrategy{}
    35→
    36→	// Create a file that we'll stage
    37→	srcDir := filepath.Join(dir, "src")
    38→	require.NoError(t, os.MkdirAll(srcDir, 0o755))
    39→	testFile := filepath.Join(srcDir, "main.go")
    40→	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(testFile, []byte("package main\n"), 0o644))
    41→
    42→	wt, err := repo.Worktree()
    43→	require.NoError(t, err)
    44→	_, err = wt.Add("src/main.go")
    45→	require.NoError(t, err)
    46→
    47→	// Get the resolved worktree path first — on macOS, t.TempDir() returns /var/...
    48→	// but git resolves symlinks to /private/var/... . Claude Code uses the resolved
    49→	// path in its transcript, so we must too.
    50→	worktreePath, err := GetWorktreePath()
    51→	require.NoError(t, err)
    52→	worktreeID, err := paths.GetWorktreeID(worktreePath)
    53→	require.NoError(t, err)
    54→
    55→	// Create a transcript file that references the file by absolute path
    56→	// (this is what Claude Code does — tool_use Write has absolute file_path)
    57→	absFilePath := filepath.Join(worktreePath, "src", "main.go")
    58→	transcriptContent := `{"type":"human","message":{"content":"write a main.go file"}}
    59→{"type":"assistant","message":{"content":[{"type":"tool_use","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"` + absFilePath + `","content":"package main\n"}}]}}
    60→`
    61→	transcriptPath := filepath.Join(dir, "transcript.jsonl")
    62→	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(transcriptPath, []byte(transcriptContent), 0o644))
    63→
    64→	// Create session state: no shadow branch (it was deleted after last condensation),
    65→	// transcript path points to the file, agent type is Claude Code
    66→	now := time.Now()
    67→
    68→	head, err := repo.Head()
    69→	require.NoError(t, err)
    70→
    71→	state := &SessionState{
    72→		SessionID:                 "test-abs-path-normalize",
    73→		BaseCommit:                head.Hash().String(),
    74→		WorktreePath:              worktreePath,
    75→		WorktreeID:                worktreeID,
    76→		StartedAt:                 now,
    77→		Phase:                     session.PhaseActive,
    78→		LastInteractionTime:       &now,
    79→		AgentType:                 agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode,
    80→		TranscriptPath:            transcriptPath,
    81→		CheckpointTranscriptStart: 0, // No prior condensation
    82→	}
    83→	require.NoError(t, s.saveSessionState(state))
    84→
    85→	// Call sessionHasNewContent — should fall through to live transcript check
    86→	// since there's no shadow branch
    87→	hasNew, err := s.sessionHasNewContent(repo, state)
    88→	require.NoError(t, err)
    89→	assert.True(t, hasNew,
    90→		"sessionHasNewContent should return true when transcript has absolute paths "+
    91→			"that match repo-relative staged files after normalization")
    92→}
    93→
    94→// TestPostCommit_NoTrailer_UpdatesBaseCommit verifies that when a commit has no
    95→// Entire-Checkpoint trailer, PostCommit still updates BaseCommit for active sessions.
    96→//
    97→// Bug: PostCommit early-returns when no trailer is found (line ~530-536). EventGitCommit
    98→// never fires, BaseCommit never updates. All subsequent commits fail the
    99→// BaseCommit == currentHeadHash filter in PrepareCommitMsg.
   100→func TestPostCommit_NoTrailer_UpdatesBaseCommit(t *testing.T) {
   101→	dir := setupGitRepo(t)
   102→	t.Chdir(dir)
   103→
   104→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(dir)
   105→	require.NoError(t, err)
   106→
   107→	s := &ManualCommitStrategy{}
   108→	sessionID := "test-postcommit-no-trailer"
   109→
   110→	// Initialize session and save a checkpoint
   111→	setupSessionWithCheckpoint(t, s, repo, dir, sessionID)
   112→
   113→	// Set phase to ACTIVE
   114→	state, err := s.loadSessionState(sessionID)
   115→	require.NoError(t, err)
   116→	state.Phase = session.PhaseActive
   117→	require.NoError(t, s.saveSessionState(state))
   118→
   119→	originalBaseCommit := state.BaseCommit
   120→
   121→	// Create a commit WITHOUT a trailer (user removed it, or mid-turn commit
   122→	// where PrepareCommitMsg couldn't add one due to Bug 1)
   123→	testFile := filepath.Join(dir, "test.txt")
   124→	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(testFile, []byte("no trailer commit"), 0o644))
   125→	wt, err := repo.Worktree()
   126→	require.NoError(t, err)
   127→	_, err = wt.Add("test.txt")
   128→	require.NoError(t, err)
   129→	_, err = wt.Commit("commit without trailer", &git.CommitOptions{
   130→		Author: &object.Signature{
   131→			Name:  "Test",
   132→			Email: "test@test.com",
   133→			When:  time.Now(),
   134→		},
   135→	})
   136→	require.NoError(t, err)
   137→
   138→	// Get the new HEAD
   139→	head, err := repo.Head()
   140→	require.NoError(t, err)
   141→	newHeadHash := head.Hash().String()
   142→	require.NotEqual(t, originalBaseCommit, newHeadHash, "HEAD should have changed")
   143→
   144→	// Run PostCommit
   145→	err = s.PostCommit()
   146→	require.NoError(t, err)
   147→
   148→	// Verify BaseCommit was updated to new HEAD
   149→	state, err = s.loadSessionState(sessionID)
   150→	require.NoError(t, err)
   151→	assert.Equal(t, newHeadHash, state.BaseCommit,
   152→		"BaseCommit should be updated to new HEAD even when commit has no trailer")
   153→
   154→	// Phase should stay ACTIVE (no state machine transition, just BaseCommit update)
   155→	assert.Equal(t, session.PhaseActive, state.Phase,
   156→		"Phase should remain ACTIVE when commit has no trailer")
   157→}
   158→
   159→// TestSaveChanges_PreservesPendingCheckpointID verifies that SaveChanges does NOT
   160→// clear PendingCheckpointID. This field is set by PostCommit for deferred condensation
   161→// and should persist through SaveChanges calls until consumed by handleTurnEndCondense.
   162→//
   163→// Bug: SaveChanges clears PendingCheckpointID at line ~120. When the agent stops,
   164→// handleTurnEndCondense finds it empty, generates a new ID, and the commit trailer
   165→// and condensed data end up with different IDs.
   166→func TestSaveChanges_PreservesPendingCheckpointID(t *testing.T) {
   167→	dir := setupGitRepo(t)
   168→	t.Chdir(dir)
   169→
   170→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(dir)
   171→	require.NoError(t, err)
   172→
   173→	s := &ManualCommitStrategy{}
   174→	sessionID := "test-preserve-pending-cpid"
   175→
   176→	// Initialize session and save first checkpoint
   177→	setupSessionWithCheckpoint(t, s, repo, dir, sessionID)
   178→
   179→	// Set PendingCheckpointID (simulating what PostCommit does)
   180→	state, err := s.loadSessionState(sessionID)
   181→	require.NoError(t, err)
   182→	const testPendingCpID = "abc123def456"
   183→	state.PendingCheckpointID = testPendingCpID
   184→	state.Phase = session.PhaseActiveCommitted
   185→	require.NoError(t, s.saveSessionState(state))
   186→
   187→	// Create metadata for a new checkpoint
   188→	metadataDir := ".entire/metadata/" + sessionID
   189→	metadataDirAbs := filepath.Join(dir, metadataDir)
   190→	// Transcript already exists from setupSessionWithCheckpoint
   191→
   192→	// Modify a file so the checkpoint has real changes
   193→	testFile := filepath.Join(dir, "src", "new_file.go")
   194→	require.NoError(t, os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(testFile), 0o755))
   195→	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(testFile, []byte("package src\n"), 0o644))
   196→
   197→	// Call SaveChanges — this should NOT clear PendingCheckpointID
   198→	err = s.SaveChanges(SaveContext{
   199→		SessionID:      sessionID,
   200→		ModifiedFiles:  []string{},
   201→		NewFiles:       []string{"src/new_file.go"},
   202→		DeletedFiles:   []string{},
   203→		MetadataDir:    metadataDir,
   204→		MetadataDirAbs: metadataDirAbs,
   205→		CommitMessage:  "Checkpoint 2",
   206→		AuthorName:     "Test",
   207→		AuthorEmail:    "test@test.com",
   208→	})
   209→	require.NoError(t, err)
   210→
   211→	// Reload state and verify PendingCheckpointID is preserved
   212→	state, err = s.loadSessionState(sessionID)
   213→	require.NoError(t, err)
   214→	assert.Equal(t, testPendingCpID, state.PendingCheckpointID,
   215→		"PendingCheckpointID should be preserved across SaveChanges calls, "+
   216→			"not cleared — it's needed for deferred condensation at turn end")
   217→}
   218→

    91→	// LastCheckpointID is the checkpoint ID from last condensation, reused for subsequent commits without new content
    92→	LastCheckpointID id.CheckpointID `json:"last_checkpoint_id,omitempty"`

    98→	TokenUsage *agent.TokenUsage `json:"token_usage,omitempty"`

   101→	// Kept for backward compatibility with existing state files.

   105→	// Used for identifier-based transcript scoping (UUID for Claude, message ID for Gemini).
   106→	TranscriptIdentifierAtStart string `json:"transcript_identifier_at_start,omitempty"`

   109→	TranscriptPath string `json:"transcript_path,omitempty"`

   112→	FirstPrompt string `json:"first_prompt,omitempty"`

   115→	// This enables accurate attribution by capturing user edits between checkpoints.
   116→	PromptAttributions []PromptAttribution `json:"prompt_attributions,omitempty"`
   117→
   118→	// PendingPromptAttribution holds attribution calculated at prompt start (before agent runs).
   119→	// This is moved to PromptAttributions when SaveChanges is called.
   120→	PendingPromptAttribution *PromptAttribution `json:"pending_prompt_attribution,omitempty"`
   121→}
   122→
   123→// PromptAttribution captures line-level attribution data at the start of each prompt.
   124→// By recording what changed since the last checkpoint BEFORE the agent works,
   125→// we can accurately separate user edits from agent contributions.
```

> AGENT

Good catch. There are two different subagent transcript path constructions, and I think one is outdated:

1. **`AgentTranscriptPath(transcriptDir, agentID)`** → `<transcriptDir>/agent-<id>.jsonl`
   - Used in `handleClaudeCodePostTask` (line 584)
   - Comment says: "stored as agent-{agentId}.jsonl in the **same directory** as the main transcript"

2. **`commitWithMetadata` token usage** → `<transcriptDir>/<sessionID>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl`
   - Used at line 325 for `CalculateTotalTokenUsage`

These point to different locations. I believe option 2 is the current Claude Code layout (it was added for the token usage calculation and is known to work). Option 1 may be an older layout. The PostTask hook has a `!fileExists` guard (line 585-587) that silently falls back, so it would fail gracefully if the path is wrong.

For this fix, I'll use the same path construction as the working token usage code (`<transcriptDir>/<sessionID>/subagents/`). If we need to support the old layout too, we can check both with a fallback.

Now let me also check `SessionState.SessionID` — I confirmed it's the model's session ID (same as `input.SessionID` from hook input), so we can construct the subagents dir from `state.TranscriptPath` and `state.SessionID` in `sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript`.

Now let me write the full implementation plan.

```
     1→# Fix Subagent File Modifications Invisible to Checkpoint Detection
     2→
     3→**Issue:** [ENT-297](https://linear.app/entirehq/issue/ENT-297/subagent-file-modifications-invisible-to-checkpoint-detection)
     4→**Date:** 2026-02-13
     5→
     6→## Problem
     7→
     8→`ExtractModifiedFiles` and related functions only parse the main agent transcript for Write/Edit/NotebookEdit tool calls. When subagents (via the `Task` tool) perform file modifications, those tool calls live in separate `agent-<id>.jsonl` transcript files and are invisible to checkpoint detection.
     9→
    10→### Impact
    11→
    12→1. **Missing checkpoints**: In the Stop hook, `totalChanges = 0` when only subagents modified files, so `SaveChanges` is skipped entirely.
    13→2. **Missing checkpoint trailers**: `sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript` also misses subagent work, so mid-session user commits get no `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer.
    14→3. **Misattribution**: Subagent file changes appear as "human" edits since they're not in the transcript-derived `ModifiedFiles` list.
    15→
    16→### Affected Code Paths
    17→
    18→| Code Path | Location | Impact |
    19→|-----------|----------|--------|
    20→| `commitWithMetadata()` | `hooks_claudecode_handlers.go:225` | `totalChanges = 0`, checkpoint skipped |
    21→| `sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript()` | `manual_commit_hooks.go:1005` | No checkpoint trailer on user commits |
    22→
    23→## Approach: Extend Transcript Parsing to Include Subagent Transcripts
    24→
    25→The pattern already exists in `CalculateTotalTokenUsage()` (`transcript.go:387-429`): parse main transcript, extract spawned agent IDs via `ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs()`, read each `agent-<id>.jsonl`, process. We apply the same pattern to file modification extraction.
    26→
    27→## Changes
    28→
    29→### 1. New function in `claudecode/transcript.go`
    30→
    31→`ExtractAllModifiedFiles(transcriptPath, startLine, subagentsDir)` combines main + subagent file modifications:
    32→
    33→- Parse main transcript from `startLine`
    34→- Call `ExtractModifiedFiles(lines)` for main agent files
    35→- Call `ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs(lines)` to find subagent IDs
    36→- For each agent ID, read and parse `<subagentsDir>/agent-<id>.jsonl`, extract modified files
    37→- Deduplicate and return merged list
    38→
    39→### 2. Update `commitWithMetadata()` in `hooks_claudecode_handlers.go`
    40→
    41→Add a CLI-layer helper `extractSubagentModifiedFiles(transcript, subagentsDir, repoRoot)` that:
    42→
    43→- Calls `claudecode.ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs()` on the parsed transcript
    44→- For each subagent, reads and parses their transcript
    45→- Returns all modified files from subagents
    46→
    47→Update line 225 to merge main + subagent modified files.
    48→
    49→### 3. Update `sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript()` in `manual_commit_hooks.go`
    50→
    51→After the existing `ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset` call, also check subagent transcripts when `AgentType == ClaudeCode`. No interface change needed — subagent-awareness stays in the strategy layer.
    52→
    53→### 4. Tests
    54→
    55→- Unit test for `ExtractAllModifiedFiles` with subagent transcripts
    56→- Unit test for `commitWithMetadata` where only subagents modify files
    57→- Unit test for `sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript` with subagent-only changes
    58→- Integration test for end-to-end subagent checkpoint creation
    59→
    60→## What This Does NOT Change
    61→
    62→- `DetectFileChanges()` — unchanged, still handles new/deleted untracked files via git status
    63→- `TranscriptAnalyzer` interface — unchanged
    64→- Gemini agent — unaffected
    65→- `handleClaudeCodePostTask()` — already correctly reads subagent transcripts
    66→
    67→## Files Touched
    68→
    69→| File | Change |
    70→|------|--------|
    71→| `agent/claudecode/transcript.go` | Add `ExtractAllModifiedFiles()` |
    72→| `hooks_claudecode_handlers.go` | Update `commitWithMetadata()` to merge subagent files |
    73→| `strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` | Update `sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript()` |
    74→| `agent/claudecode/transcript_test.go` | New unit tests |
    75→| `strategy/mid_turn_commit_test.go` | New test for live transcript check |
    76→| `integration_test/` | New integration test |
    77→
```

> AGENT

Plan saved. Let me answer your question about the two paths.

Both paths refer to the **original agent transcript file** (the live Claude Code transcript), not our metadata storage:

1. **`AgentTranscriptPath`** (PostTask hook): `filepath.Dir(input.TranscriptPath) + "/agent-<id>.jsonl"` — assumes subagent transcripts are siblings of the main transcript
2. **Token usage code** (Stop hook): `filepath.Dir(transcriptPath) + "/<sessionID>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl"` — puts them in a session-scoped subdirectory

Neither is our shadow branch or `.entire/metadata` storage. Both are pointing at Claude Code's own transcript directory (typically `~/.claude/projects/<hash>/`). The question is just: does Claude Code put subagent transcripts flat next to `transcript.jsonl`, or nested in `<sessionID>/subagents/`?

The token usage code (option 2) is known to work for counting tokens, so it's the correct path for current Claude Code versions. `AgentTranscriptPath` might be from an older layout or might just silently fail via the `!fileExists` guard.

---

Plan is complete and saved to `docs/plans/2026-02-13-subagent-checkpoint-implementation.md`. Two execution options:

**1. Subagent-Driven (this session)** — I dispatch a fresh subagent per task, review between tasks, fast iteration

**2. Parallel Session (separate)** — Open new session with executing-plans, batch execution with checkpoints

> AGENT

That confirms it. The actual layout is:

```
<project-dir>/
├── 23ce4bbe-...jsonl              # Main transcript
└── 23ce4bbe-.../                  # Session subdirectory
    └── subagents/
        └── agent-a555565.jsonl    # Subagent transcript
```

So the **token usage code** has the correct path: `filepath.Dir(transcriptPath) + "/" + sessionID + "/subagents/"`. And `AgentTranscriptPath` (which puts it flat next to the main transcript) is wrong — it would look for `<project-dir>/agent-a555565.jsonl` which doesn't exist. It silently fails via the `!fileExists` guard in PostTask.

That means `handleClaudeCodePostTask` has likely **never** successfully read a subagent transcript via that path. It falls through to the main transcript fallback at line 604-610. Good to know — not a regression from our change, but a pre-existing bug worth noting.

The implementation plan already uses the correct path construction. Want to proceed with execution? Subagent-driven (this session) or parallel session?

Here is the summary of what was done for Task 1:

---

## Summary

### What was implemented

**New function** `ExtractAllModifiedFiles` in `/Users/alex/workspace/cli/.worktrees/2/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/transcript.go` (lines 431-475):

```go
func ExtractAllModifiedFiles(transcriptPath string, startLine int, subagentsDir string) ([]string, error)
```

This function extracts files modified by both the main agent and any subagents spawned via the Task tool. It:
1. Parses the main transcript from `startLine` using `parseTranscriptFromLine()`
2. Calls `ExtractModifiedFiles()` for the main agent's files
3. Calls `ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs()` to find subagent IDs
4. For each agent ID, reads `<subagentsDir>/agent-<id>.jsonl`, parses it, and extracts modified files
5. Deduplicates all files across agents
6. Skips missing subagent transcripts gracefully (no error)

The implementation follows the exact same pattern as `CalculateTotalTokenUsage` (line 387-429 of the same file).

### What tests were added

4 tests in `/Users/alex/workspace/cli/.worktrees/2/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/transcript_test.go`:

1. **`TestExtractAllModifiedFiles_IncludesSubagentFiles`** - Main writes to `main.go`, subagent writes to `helper.go` and edits `utils.go` -- returns all 3 files
2. **`TestExtractAllModifiedFiles_DeduplicatesAcrossAgents`** - Main and subagent both modify `shared.go` -- appears only once
3. **`TestExtractAllModifiedFiles_NoSubagents`** - Main only writes a file, no Task calls -- works normally
4. **`TestExtractAllModifiedFiles_SubagentOnlyChanges`** - Main ONLY has Task calls (no Write/Edit), subagent writes 2 files -- returns the subagent's 2 files (the key bug scenario for ENT-297)

5 test helpers were also added (`writeJSONLFile`, `makeWriteToolLine`, `makeEditToolLine`, `makeTaskToolUseLine`, `makeTaskResultLine`) to construct JSONL fixtures cleanly.

### Decisions and trade-offs

- Followed the existing test style (standard Go testing, no testify) to match the file's conventions
- The `ExtractModifiedFiles` already deduplicates internally, but we maintain our own `fileSet` across agents for cross-agent deduplication
- Missing subagent transcripts are silently skipped, matching the behavior in `CalculateTotalTokenUsage`

### Verification

- All 4 new tests pass
- All existing tests in the package pass
- `mise run fmt` -- clean
- `mise run lint` -- 0 issues

   430→
   431→// ExtractAllModifiedFiles extracts files modified by both the main agent and
   432→// any subagents spawned via the Task tool. It parses the main transcript from
   433→// startLine, collects modified files from the main agent, then reads each
   434→// subagent's transcript from subagentsDir to collect their modified files too.
   435→// The result is a deduplicated list of all modified file paths.
   436→func ExtractAllModifiedFiles(transcriptPath string, startLine int, subagentsDir string) ([]string, error) {
   437→	if transcriptPath == "" {
   438→		return nil, nil
   439→	}
   440→
   441→	// Parse main transcript once
   442→	transcript, err := parseTranscriptFromLine(transcriptPath, startLine)
   443→	if err != nil {
   444→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse transcript: %w", err)
   445→	}
   446→
   447→	// Collect modified files from main agent
   448→	fileSet := make(map[string]bool)
   449→	var files []string
   450→	for _, f := range ExtractModifiedFiles(transcript) {
   451→		if !fileSet[f] {
   452→			fileSet[f] = true
   453→			files = append(files, f)
   454→		}
   455→	}
   456→
   457→	// Find spawned subagents and collect their modified files
   458→	agentIDs := ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs(transcript)
   459→	for agentID := range agentIDs {
   460→		agentPath := filepath.Join(subagentsDir, fmt.Sprintf("agent-%s.jsonl", agentID))
   461→		agentTranscript, err := parseTranscriptFromLine(agentPath, 0)
   462→		if err != nil {
   463→			// Subagent transcript may not exist yet or may have been cleaned up
   464→			continue
   465→		}
   466→		for _, f := range ExtractModifiedFiles(agentTranscript) {
   467→			if !fileSet[f] {
   468→				fileSet[f] = true
   469→				files = append(files, f)
   470→			}
   471→		}
   472→	}
   473→
   474→	return files, nil
   475→}
   476→

   623→// writeJSONLFile is a test helper that writes JSONL transcript lines to a file.
   624→func writeJSONLFile(t *testing.T, path string, lines ...string) {
   625→	t.Helper()
   626→	var buf strings.Builder
   627→	for _, line := range lines {
   628→		buf.WriteString(line)
   629→		buf.WriteByte('\n')
   630→	}
   631→	if err := os.WriteFile(path, []byte(buf.String()), 0o600); err != nil {
   632→		t.Fatalf("failed to write JSONL file %s: %v", path, err)
   633→	}
   634→}
   635→

   732→		makeWriteToolLine(t, "a1", "/repo/main.go"),
   733→		makeTaskToolUseLine(t, "a2", "toolu_task1"),
   734→		makeTaskResultLine(t, "u1", "toolu_task1", "sub1"),
   735→	)

   737→	// Subagent transcript: Write to helper.go + Edit to utils.go

   739→		makeWriteToolLine(t, "sa1", "/repo/helper.go"),
   740→		makeEditToolLine(t, "sa2", "/repo/utils.go"),
   741→	)
   742→
   743→	files, err := ExtractAllModifiedFiles(transcriptPath, 0, subagentsDir)

   745→		t.Fatalf("ExtractAllModifiedFiles() error: %v", err)
   746→	}
   747→
   748→	if len(files) != 3 {
   749→		t.Errorf("expected 3 files, got %d: %v", len(files), files)

   751→
   752→	wantFiles := map[string]bool{

   754→		"/repo/helper.go": true,
   755→		"/repo/utils.go":  true,
   756→	}
   757→	for _, f := range files {

   760→		}
   761→		delete(wantFiles, f)

We need to replace this with a call to `claudecode.ExtractAllModifiedFiles` which includes subagent transcripts.\n\n**The change:**\n\n1. At line 225, replace the single `extractModifiedFiles(transcript)` call with `claudecode.ExtractAllModifiedFiles`. The subagent directory path follows the Claude Code layout: `<transcriptDir>/<modelSessionID>/subagents/`. Use `input.SessionID` (the model's raw session ID) for path construction, NOT the `sessionID` variable (which may have been overridden to `unknownSessionID`).\n\n2. Add a fallback: if `ExtractAllModifiedFiles` fails, fall back to the original `extractModifiedFiles(transcript)`.\n\n3. At line ~325, there's already a `subagentsDir` variable defined for token usage. Since we now define `subagentsDir` earlier (for file extraction), we need to reuse it rather than redefine it. Change line 325 from `subagentsDir := ...` to just reuse the variable.\n\n**Concrete code change at line 225:**\n\n```go\n// REPLACE THIS (line 225):\nmodifiedFiles := extractModifiedFiles(transcript)\n\n// WITH THIS:\n// Get modified files from transcript (main agent + subagents).\n// Subagent transcripts live in <transcriptDir>/<modelSessionID>/subagents/\nsubagentsDir := filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(transcriptPath), input.SessionID, \"subagents\")\nmodifiedFiles, subagentErr := claudecode.ExtractAllModifiedFiles(transcriptPath, transcriptOffset, subagentsDir)\nif subagentErr != nil {\n    fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Warning: failed to extract modified files with subagents: %v\\n\", subagentErr)\n    // Fall back to main transcript only\n    modifiedFiles = extractModifiedFiles(transcript)\n}\n```\n\n**And at line ~325 (token usage section):**\n\n```go\n// REPLACE THIS (line ~325):\nsubagentsDir := filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(transcriptPath), sessionID, \"subagents\")\nusage, err := claudecode.CalculateTotalTokenUsage(transcriptPath, transcriptLinesAtStart, subagentsDir)\n\n// WITH THIS (reuse subagentsDir from above):\nusage, tokenErr := claudecode.CalculateTotalTokenUsage(transcriptPath, transcriptLinesAtStart, subagentsDir)\n```\n\nNote: rename the `err` to `tokenErr` to avoid shadowing.\n\n**Import:** The file already imports `\"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode\"` — verify this is present.\n\n## Context\n\nThis is the core fix for ENT-297. Task 2 is complete. Here is a summary of what was done:

## Changes Made

**File:** `/Users/alex/workspace/cli/.worktrees/2/cmd/entire/cli/hooks_claudecode_handlers.go`

### Change 1 (line 224-232): Subagent-aware file extraction

Replaced the single-transcript `extractModifiedFiles(transcript)` call with `claudecode.ExtractAllModifiedFiles`, which:
- Constructs the subagents directory path using `input.SessionID` (the model's raw session ID, not the potentially-overridden `sessionID` variable)
- Parses both the main transcript and all subagent transcripts to build a merged list of modified files
- Falls back gracefully to `extractModifiedFiles(transcript)` if the new function fails

```go
subagentsDir := filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(transcriptPath), input.SessionID, "subagents")
modifiedFiles, subagentErr := claudecode.ExtractAllModifiedFiles(transcriptPath, transcriptOffset, subagentsDir)
if subagentErr != nil {
    fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to extract modified files with subagents: %v\n", subagentErr)
    modifiedFiles = extractModifiedFiles(transcript)
}
```

### Change 2 (line 331): Reuse subagentsDir, rename error variable

- Removed the duplicate `subagentsDir` definition that was previously at the token usage section
- Renamed `err` to `tokenErr` to avoid variable shadowing with the outer scope `err`

```go
usage, tokenErr := claudecode.CalculateTotalTokenUsage(transcriptPath, transcriptLinesAtStart, subagentsDir)
if tokenErr != nil {
    fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to calculate token usage: %v\n", tokenErr)
```

### Verification
- `mise run fmt` -- clean
- `mise run lint` -- 0 issues
- `go test ./cmd/entire/cli/... -count=1` -- all 21 packages pass

Done. Here is the summary of what was implemented:

## Summary

### Test Added (TDD Red -> Green)

**File:** `/Users/alex/workspace/cli/.worktrees/2/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/mid_turn_commit_test.go`

Added `TestSessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript_IncludesSubagentFiles` which:
1. Sets up a git repo with a staged file (`src/feature.go`)
2. Creates a main transcript with ONLY a Task tool call and its result (no direct Write/Edit)
3. Creates a subagent transcript at `<transcriptDir>/<sessionID>/subagents/agent-sub123.jsonl` with a Write tool_use targeting the staged file
4. Creates a `SessionState` with `AgentType=ClaudeCode` and `CheckpointTranscriptStart=0`
5. Calls `s.sessionHasNewContent(repo, state)` and asserts it returns `true`

The test does NOT use `t.Parallel()` because it calls `t.Chdir()` which modifies process-global state.

### Code Change

**File:** `/Users/alex/workspace/cli/.worktrees/2/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go`

In `sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript`, after the existing `ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset` call (which only scans the main transcript), added a check that also scans subagent transcripts when `AgentType == ClaudeCode`:

```go
if state.AgentType == agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode && state.TranscriptPath != "" {
    subagentsDir := filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(state.TranscriptPath), state.SessionID, "subagents")
    allFiles, extractErr := claudecode.ExtractAllModifiedFiles(
        state.TranscriptPath, state.CheckpointTranscriptStart, subagentsDir,
    )
    if extractErr == nil && len(allFiles) > len(modifiedFiles) {
        modifiedFiles = allFiles
    }
}
```

Also added `"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode"` to the imports.

### Validation
- Both `TestSessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript_NormalizesAbsolutePaths` (existing) and `TestSessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript_IncludesSubagentFiles` (new) pass
- All strategy tests pass
- `mise run fmt` and `mise run lint` pass cleanly (0 issues)

   134→**Step 3: Write minimal implementation**
   135→
   136→Add to `cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/transcript.go` after line 429:
   137→
   138→```go
   139→// ExtractAllModifiedFiles extracts files modified by both the main agent and any
   140→// subagents spawned via the Task tool. It follows the same pattern as
   141→// CalculateTotalTokenUsage: parse main transcript, extract spawned agent IDs,
   142→// then read each subagent transcript.
   143→//
   144→// Parameters:
   145→//   - transcriptPath: path to the main agent's JSONL transcript
   146→//   - startLine: line offset to start parsing from (0 = beginning)
   147→//   - subagentsDir: directory containing subagent transcripts (agent-<id>.jsonl)
   148→//
   149→// Returns deduplicated list of file paths modified by any agent.
   150→func ExtractAllModifiedFiles(transcriptPath string, startLine int, subagentsDir string) ([]string, error) {
   151→	if transcriptPath == "" {
   152→		return nil, nil
   153→	}
   154→
   155→	// Parse main transcript
   156→	transcript, err := parseTranscriptFromLine(transcriptPath, startLine)
   157→	if err != nil {
   158→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse transcript: %w", err)
   159→	}
   160→
   161→	// Extract files from main agent
   162→	fileSet := make(map[string]bool)
   163→	var files []string
   164→	for _, f := range ExtractModifiedFiles(transcript) {
   165→		if !fileSet[f] {
   166→			fileSet[f] = true
   167→			files = append(files, f)
   168→		}
   169→	}
   170→
   171→	// Extract spawned agent IDs and scan their transcripts
   172→	agentIDs := ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs(transcript)
   173→	for agentID := range agentIDs {
   174→		agentPath := filepath.Join(subagentsDir, fmt.Sprintf("agent-%s.jsonl", agentID))
   175→		agentTranscript, readErr := parseTranscriptFromLine(agentPath, 0)
   176→		if readErr != nil {
   177→			continue // Subagent transcript may not exist or may have been cleaned up
   178→		}
   179→		for _, f := range ExtractModifiedFiles(agentTranscript) {
   180→			if !fileSet[f] {
   181→				fileSet[f] = true
   182→				files = append(files, f)
   183→			}
   184→		}
   185→	}
   186→
   187→	return files, nil
   188→}
   189→```

   209→### Task 2: Update `commitWithMetadata` Stop Hook to Include Subagent Files
   210→
   211→**Files:**
   212→- Modify: `cmd/entire/cli/hooks_claudecode_handlers.go:225`
   213→
   214→**Context:**
   215→The `subagentsDir` is already computed at line 325 for token usage: `filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(transcriptPath), sessionID, "subagents")`. We need the same directory earlier for file extraction. Note: use `input.SessionID` (model session ID) for path construction since that's what Claude Code uses for directory naming.
   216→
   217→**Step 1: Implement the change**
   218→
   219→In `cmd/entire/cli/hooks_claudecode_handlers.go`, replace line 225:
   220→
   221→```go
   222→// BEFORE (line 225):
   223→modifiedFiles := extractModifiedFiles(transcript)
   224→
   225→// AFTER:
   226→// Get modified files from transcript (main agent + subagents).
   227→// Subagent transcripts live in <transcriptDir>/<modelSessionID>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl
   228→subagentsDir := filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(transcriptPath), input.SessionID, "subagents")
   229→modifiedFiles, subagentErr := claudecode.ExtractAllModifiedFiles(transcriptPath, transcriptOffset, subagentsDir)
   230→if subagentErr != nil {

   392→		return false, nil
   393→	}

    30→// testBinaryPath holds the path to the CLI binary built once in TestMain.
    31→// All tests share this binary to avoid repeated builds.
    32→var testBinaryPath string
    33→
    34→// getTestBinary returns the path to the shared test binary.
    35→// It panics if TestMain hasn't run (testBinaryPath is empty).
    36→func getTestBinary() string {
    37→	if testBinaryPath == "" {
    38→		panic("testBinaryPath not set - TestMain must run before tests")
    39→	}
    40→	return testBinaryPath
    41→}
    42→
    43→// TestEnv manages an isolated test environment for integration tests.
    44→type TestEnv struct {
    45→	T                *testing.T
    46→	RepoDir          string
    47→	ClaudeProjectDir string
    48→	GeminiProjectDir string
    49→	SessionCounter   int
    50→}
    51→
    52→// NewTestEnv creates a new isolated test environment.
    53→// It creates temp directories for the git repo and agent project files.
    54→// Note: Does NOT change working directory to allow parallel test execution.
    55→// Note: Does NOT use t.Setenv to allow parallel test execution - CLI commands
    56→// receive the env var via cmd.Env instead.
    57→func NewTestEnv(t *testing.T) *TestEnv {
    58→	t.Helper()
    59→
    60→	// Resolve symlinks on macOS where /var -> /private/var
    61→	// This ensures the CLI subprocess and test use consistent paths
    62→	repoDir := t.TempDir()
    63→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(repoDir); err == nil {
    64→		repoDir = resolved
    65→	}
    66→	claudeProjectDir := t.TempDir()
    67→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(claudeProjectDir); err == nil {
    68→		claudeProjectDir = resolved
    69→	}
    70→	geminiProjectDir := t.TempDir()
    71→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(geminiProjectDir); err == nil {
    72→		geminiProjectDir = resolved
    73→	}
    74→
    75→	env := &TestEnv{
    76→		T:                t,
    77→		RepoDir:          repoDir,
    78→		ClaudeProjectDir: claudeProjectDir,
    79→		GeminiProjectDir: geminiProjectDir,
    80→	}
    81→
    82→	// Note: Don't use t.Setenv here - it's incompatible with t.Parallel()
    83→	// CLI commands receive ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR or ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR via cmd.Env instead
    84→
    85→	return env
    86→}
    87→
    88→// Cleanup is a no-op retained for backwards compatibility.
    89→//
    90→// Previously this method restored the working directory after NewTestEnv changed it.
    91→// With the refactor to remove os.Chdir from NewTestEnv:
    92→// - Temp directories are now cleaned up automatically by t.TempDir()
    93→// - Working directory is never changed, so no restoration is needed
    94→//
    95→// This method is kept to avoid breaking existing tests that call defer env.Cleanup().
    96→// New tests should not call this method as it serves no purpose.
    97→//
    98→// Deprecated: This method is a no-op and will be removed in a future version.
    99→func (env *TestEnv) Cleanup() {
   100→	// No-op - temp dirs are cleaned up by t.TempDir()
   101→}
   102→
   103→// cliEnv returns the environment variables for CLI execution.
   104→// Includes both Claude and Gemini project dirs so tests work for any agent.
   105→func (env *TestEnv) cliEnv() []string {
   106→	return append(os.Environ(),
   107→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+env.ClaudeProjectDir,
   108→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+env.GeminiProjectDir,
   109→	)
   110→}

   112→// RunCLI runs the entire CLI with the given arguments and returns stdout.
   113→func (env *TestEnv) RunCLI(args ...string) string {
   114→	env.T.Helper()
   115→	output, err := env.RunCLIWithError(args...)
   116→	if err != nil {
   117→		env.T.Fatalf("CLI command failed: %v\nArgs: %v\nOutput: %s", err, args, output)
   118→	}
   119→	return output
   120→}
   121→
   122→// RunCLIWithError runs the entire CLI and returns output and error.
   123→func (env *TestEnv) RunCLIWithError(args ...string) (string, error) {
   124→	env.T.Helper()
   125→
   126→	// Run CLI using the shared binary
   127→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), args...)
   128→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   129→	cmd.Env = env.cliEnv()
   130→
   131→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   132→	return string(output), err
   133→}

   138→
   139→	// Run CLI with stdin using the shared binary
   140→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), args...)
   141→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   142→	cmd.Env = env.cliEnv()
   143→	cmd.Stdin = strings.NewReader(stdin)
   144→
   145→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()

   148→	}
   149→	return string(output)
   150→}
   151→
   152→// NewRepoEnv creates a TestEnv with an initialized git repo and Entire.
   153→// This is a convenience factory for tests that need a basic repo setup.
   154→func NewRepoEnv(t *testing.T, strategy string) *TestEnv {
   155→	t.Helper()
   156→	env := NewTestEnv(t)
   157→	env.InitRepo()
   158→	env.InitEntire(strategy)
   159→	return env
   160→}
   161→
   162→// NewRepoWithCommit creates a TestEnv with a git repo, Entire, and an initial commit.
   163→// The initial commit contains a README.md file.
   164→func NewRepoWithCommit(t *testing.T, strategy string) *TestEnv {
   165→	t.Helper()
   166→	env := NewRepoEnv(t, strategy)
   167→	env.WriteFile("README.md", "# Test Repository")
   168→	env.GitAdd("README.md")
   169→	env.GitCommit("Initial commit")
   170→	return env
   171→}
   172→
   173→// NewFeatureBranchEnv creates a TestEnv ready for session testing.
   174→// It initializes the repo, creates an initial commit on main,
   175→// and checks out a feature branch. This is the most common setup
   176→// for session and rewind tests since Entire tracking skips main/master.
   177→func NewFeatureBranchEnv(t *testing.T, strategyName string) *TestEnv {
   178→	t.Helper()
   179→	env := NewRepoWithCommit(t, strategyName)
   180→	env.GitCheckoutNewBranch("feature/test-branch")
   181→	return env
   182→}
   183→
   184→// AllStrategies returns all strategy names for parameterized tests.
   185→func AllStrategies() []string {
   186→	return []string{
   187→		strategy.StrategyNameAutoCommit,
   188→		strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit,
   189→	}
   190→}
   191→
   192→// RunForAllStrategies runs a test function for each strategy in parallel.
   193→// This reduces boilerplate for tests that need to verify behavior across all strategies.
   194→// Each subtest gets its own TestEnv with a feature branch ready for testing.
   195→func RunForAllStrategies(t *testing.T, testFn func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string)) {
   196→	t.Helper()
   197→	for _, strat := range AllStrategies() {
   198→		strat := strat // capture for parallel
   199→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   200→			t.Parallel()
   201→			env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, strat)
   202→			testFn(t, env, strat)
   203→		})
   204→	}
   205→}
   206→
   207→// RunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv runs a test function for each strategy in parallel,
   208→// using NewRepoWithCommit instead of NewFeatureBranchEnv. Use this for tests
   209→// that need to test behavior on the main branch.
   210→func RunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv(t *testing.T, testFn func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string)) {
   211→	t.Helper()
   212→	for _, strat := range AllStrategies() {
   213→		strat := strat // capture for parallel
   214→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   215→			t.Parallel()
   216→			env := NewRepoWithCommit(t, strat)
   217→			testFn(t, env, strat)
   218→		})
   219→	}
   220→}
   221→
   222→// RunForAllStrategiesWithBasicEnv runs a test function for each strategy in parallel,
   223→// using NewRepoEnv (git repo + entire init, no commits). Use this for tests
   224→// that need to verify basic initialization behavior.
   225→func RunForAllStrategiesWithBasicEnv(t *testing.T, testFn func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string)) {
   226→	t.Helper()
   227→	for _, strat := range AllStrategies() {
   228→		strat := strat // capture for parallel
   229→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   230→			t.Parallel()
   231→			env := NewRepoEnv(t, strat)
   232→			testFn(t, env, strat)
   233→		})
   234→	}
   235→}
   236→
   237→// RunForStrategiesSequential runs a test function for specific strategies sequentially.
   238→// Use this for tests that cannot be parallelized (e.g., tests using os.Chdir).
   239→// The strategies parameter allows testing a subset of strategies.
   240→func RunForStrategiesSequential(t *testing.T, strategies []string, testFn func(t *testing.T, strategyName string)) {
   241→	t.Helper()
   242→	for _, strat := range strategies {
   243→		t.Run(strat, func(t *testing.T) {
   244→			testFn(t, strat)
   245→		})
   246→	}
   247→}
   248→
   249→// InitRepo initializes a git repository in the test environment.
   250→func (env *TestEnv) InitRepo() {
   251→	env.T.Helper()
   252→
   253→	repo, err := git.PlainInit(env.RepoDir, false)
   254→	if err != nil {
   255→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to init git repo: %v", err)
   256→	}
   257→
   258→	// Configure git user for commits
   259→	cfg, err := repo.Config()
   260→	if err != nil {
   261→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get repo config: %v", err)
   262→	}
   263→	cfg.User.Name = "Test User"
   264→	cfg.User.Email = "test@example.com"
   265→
   266→	// Disable GPG signing for test commits (prevents failures if user has commit.gpgsign=true globally)
   267→	if cfg.Raw == nil {
   268→		cfg.Raw = config.New()
   269→	}
   270→	cfg.Raw.Section("commit").SetOption("gpgsign", "false")
   271→
   272→	if err := repo.SetConfig(cfg); err != nil {
   273→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to set repo config: %v", err)
   274→	}
   275→}
   276→
   277→// InitEntire initializes the .entire directory with the specified strategy.
   278→func (env *TestEnv) InitEntire(strategyName string) {

   280→}
   281→
   282→// InitEntireWithOptions initializes the .entire directory with the specified strategy and options.
   283→func (env *TestEnv) InitEntireWithOptions(strategyName string, strategyOptions map[string]any) {
   284→	env.T.Helper()
   285→	env.initEntireInternal(strategyName, "", strategyOptions)
   286→}
   287→
   288→// InitEntireWithAgent initializes an Entire test environment with a specific agent.
   289→// If agentName is empty, defaults to claude-code.
   290→func (env *TestEnv) InitEntireWithAgent(strategyName string, agentName agent.AgentName) {
   291→	env.T.Helper()
   292→	env.initEntireInternal(strategyName, agentName, nil)
   293→}
   294→
   295→// InitEntireWithAgentAndOptions initializes Entire with the specified strategy, agent, and options.
   296→func (env *TestEnv) InitEntireWithAgentAndOptions(strategyName string, agentName agent.AgentName, strategyOptions map[string]any) {
   297→	env.T.Helper()
   298→	env.initEntireInternal(strategyName, agentName, strategyOptions)
   299→}
   300→
   301→// initEntireInternal is the common implementation for InitEntire variants.
   302→func (env *TestEnv) initEntireInternal(strategyName string, agentName agent.AgentName, strategyOptions map[string]any) {
   303→	env.T.Helper()
   304→
   305→	// Create .entire directory structure
   306→	entireDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".entire")
   307→	if err := os.MkdirAll(entireDir, 0o755); err != nil {
   308→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to create .entire directory: %v", err)
   309→	}
   310→
   311→	// Create tmp directory
   312→	tmpDir := filepath.Join(entireDir, "tmp")
   313→	if err := os.MkdirAll(tmpDir, 0o755); err != nil {
   314→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to create .entire/tmp directory: %v", err)
   315→	}
   316→
   317→	// Write settings.json
   318→	settings := map[string]any{
   319→		"strategy":  strategyName,
   320→		"local_dev": true, // Note: git-triggered hooks won't work (path is relative); tests call hooks via getTestBinary() instead
   321→	}
   322→	// Only add agent if specified (otherwise defaults to claude-code)
   323→	if agentName != "" {
   324→		settings["agent"] = string(agentName)
   325→	}
   326→	if strategyOptions != nil {
   327→		settings["strategy_options"] = strategyOptions
   328→	}
   329→	data, err := jsonutil.MarshalIndentWithNewline(settings, "", "  ")
   330→	if err != nil {
   331→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to marshal settings: %v", err)
   332→	}
   333→	settingsPath := filepath.Join(entireDir, paths.SettingsFileName)
   334→	if err := os.WriteFile(settingsPath, data, 0o644); err != nil {
   335→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write %s: %v", paths.SettingsFileName, err)
   336→	}
   337→}
   338→
   339→// WriteFile creates a file with the given content in the test repo.
   340→// It creates parent directories as needed.
   341→func (env *TestEnv) WriteFile(path, content string) {
   342→	env.T.Helper()
   343→
   344→	fullPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, path)
   345→
   346→	// Create parent directories
   347→	dir := filepath.Dir(fullPath)
   348→	if err := os.MkdirAll(dir, 0o755); err != nil {
   349→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to create directory %s: %v", dir, err)
   350→	}
   351→
   352→	if err := os.WriteFile(fullPath, []byte(content), 0o644); err != nil {
   353→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write file %s: %v", path, err)
   354→	}
   355→}

   496→// GitCommitWithMultipleSessions creates a commit with multiple Entire-Session trailers.
   497→// This simulates merge commits that combine work from multiple sessions.

   502→	fullMessage := message + "\n\n"
   503→	var fullMessageSb404 strings.Builder
   504→	for _, sessionID := range sessionIDs {
   505→		fullMessageSb404.WriteString("Entire-Session: " + sessionID + "\n")
   506→	}
   507→	fullMessage += fullMessageSb404.String()
   508→
   509→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   510→	if err != nil {

   512→	}

   515→	if err != nil {

   517→	}
   518→
   519→	_, err = worktree.Commit(fullMessage, &git.CommitOptions{
   520→		Author: &object.Signature{
   521→			Name:  "Test User",
   522→			Email: "test@example.com",
   523→			When:  time.Now(),
   524→		},
   525→	})
   526→	if err != nil {
   527→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
   528→	}
   529→}

   573→// GetGitLog returns a list of commit hashes from HEAD.
   574→func (env *TestEnv) GetGitLog() []string {
   575→	env.T.Helper()
   576→
   577→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   578→	if err != nil {
   579→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   580→	}
   581→
   582→	head, err := repo.Head()
   583→	if err != nil {
   584→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
   585→	}
   586→
   587→	commitIter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: head.Hash()})
   588→	if err != nil {
   589→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get log: %v", err)
   590→	}
   591→
   592→	var commits []string
   593→	err = commitIter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error {
   594→		commits = append(commits, c.Hash.String())
   595→		return nil
   596→	})
   597→	if err != nil {
   598→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to iterate commits: %v", err)
   599→	}
   600→
   601→	return commits
   602→}

   604→// GitCheckoutNewBranch creates and checks out a new branch.
   605→// Uses git CLI instead of go-git to work around go-git v5 bug where Checkout
   606→// deletes untracked files (see https://github.com/go-git/go-git/issues/970).
   607→func (env *TestEnv) GitCheckoutNewBranch(branchName string) {
   608→	env.T.Helper()
   609→
   610→	cmd := exec.Command("git", "checkout", "-b", branchName)
   611→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   612→	if output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   613→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to checkout new branch %s: %v\nOutput: %s", branchName, err, output)
   614→	}
   615→}
   616→
   617→// GetCurrentBranch returns the current branch name.
   618→func (env *TestEnv) GetCurrentBranch() string {
   619→	env.T.Helper()
   620→
   621→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   622→	if err != nil {
   623→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   624→	}
   625→
   626→	head, err := repo.Head()
   627→	if err != nil {
   628→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
   629→	}
   630→
   631→	if !head.Name().IsBranch() {
   632→		return "" // Detached HEAD
   633→	}
   634→
   635→	return head.Name().Short()
   636→}

   638→// RewindPoint mirrors strategy.RewindPoint for test assertions.
   639→type RewindPoint struct {
   640→	ID               string
   641→	Message          string
   642→	MetadataDir      string
   643→	Date             time.Time
   644→	IsTaskCheckpoint bool
   645→	ToolUseID        string
   646→	IsLogsOnly       bool
   647→	CondensationID   string
   648→}
   649→
   650→// GetRewindPoints returns available rewind points using the CLI.
   651→func (env *TestEnv) GetRewindPoints() []RewindPoint {
   652→	env.T.Helper()
   653→
   654→	// Run rewind --list using the shared binary
   655→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "rewind", "--list")
   656→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   657→	cmd.Env = env.cliEnv()
   658→
   659→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   660→	if err != nil {
   661→		env.T.Fatalf("rewind --list failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   662→	}
   663→
   664→	// Parse JSON output
   665→	var jsonPoints []struct {
   666→		ID               string `json:"id"`
   667→		Message          string `json:"message"`
   668→		MetadataDir      string `json:"metadata_dir"`
   669→		Date             string `json:"date"`
   670→		IsTaskCheckpoint bool   `json:"is_task_checkpoint"`
   671→		ToolUseID        string `json:"tool_use_id"`
   672→		IsLogsOnly       bool   `json:"is_logs_only"`
   673→		CondensationID   string `json:"condensation_id"`
   674→	}
   675→
   676→	if err := json.Unmarshal(output, &jsonPoints); err != nil {
   677→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to parse rewind points: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   678→	}
   679→
   680→	points := make([]RewindPoint, len(jsonPoints))
   681→	for i, jp := range jsonPoints {
   682→		date, _ := time.Parse(time.RFC3339, jp.Date)
   683→		points[i] = RewindPoint{
   684→			ID:               jp.ID,
   685→			Message:          jp.Message,
   686→			MetadataDir:      jp.MetadataDir,
   687→			Date:             date,
   688→			IsTaskCheckpoint: jp.IsTaskCheckpoint,
   689→			ToolUseID:        jp.ToolUseID,
   690→			IsLogsOnly:       jp.IsLogsOnly,
   691→			CondensationID:   jp.CondensationID,
   692→		}
   693→	}
   694→
   695→	return points
   696→}
   697→
   698→// Rewind performs a rewind to the specified commit ID using the CLI.
   699→func (env *TestEnv) Rewind(commitID string) error {
   700→	env.T.Helper()
   701→
   702→	// Run rewind --to <commitID> using the shared binary
   703→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "rewind", "--to", commitID)
   704→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   705→	cmd.Env = env.cliEnv()
   706→
   707→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   708→	if err != nil {
   709→		return errors.New("rewind failed: " + string(output))
   710→	}
   711→
   712→	env.T.Logf("Rewind output: %s", output)
   713→	return nil
   714→}

   754→// BranchExists checks if a branch exists in the repository.
   755→func (env *TestEnv) BranchExists(branchName string) bool {
   756→	env.T.Helper()
   757→
   758→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   759→	if err != nil {
   760→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   761→	}
   762→
   763→	refs, err := repo.References()
   764→	if err != nil {
   765→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get references: %v", err)
   766→	}
   767→
   768→	found := false
   769→	_ = refs.ForEach(func(ref *plumbing.Reference) error {
   770→		if ref.Name().Short() == branchName {
   771→			found = true
   772→		}
   773→		return nil
   774→	})
   775→
   776→	return found
   777→}

   779→// GetCommitMessage returns the commit message for the given commit hash.
   780→func (env *TestEnv) GetCommitMessage(hash string) string {
   781→	env.T.Helper()
   782→
   783→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   784→	if err != nil {
   785→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   786→	}
   787→
   788→	commitHash := plumbing.NewHash(hash)
   789→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(commitHash)
   790→	if err != nil {
   791→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get commit %s: %v", hash, err)
   792→	}
   793→
   794→	return commit.Message
   795→}
   796→
   797→// FileExistsInBranch checks if a file exists in a specific branch's tree.
   798→func (env *TestEnv) FileExistsInBranch(branchName, filePath string) bool {
   799→	env.T.Helper()
   800→
   801→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   802→	if err != nil {
   803→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   804→	}
   805→
   806→	// Get the branch reference
   807→	ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName), true)
   808→	if err != nil {
   809→		// Try as a remote-style ref
   810→		ref, err = repo.Reference(plumbing.ReferenceName("refs/heads/"+branchName), true)
   811→		if err != nil {
   812→			return false
   813→		}
   814→	}
   815→
   816→	// Get the commit
   817→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   818→	if err != nil {
   819→		return false
   820→	}
   821→
   822→	// Get the tree
   823→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   824→	if err != nil {
   825→		return false
   826→	}
   827→
   828→	// Check if file exists
   829→	_, err = tree.File(filePath)
   830→	return err == nil
   831→}
   832→
   833→// ReadFileFromBranch reads a file's content from a specific branch's tree.
   834→// Returns the content and true if found, empty string and false if not found.
   835→func (env *TestEnv) ReadFileFromBranch(branchName, filePath string) (string, bool) {
   836→	env.T.Helper()
   837→
   838→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   839→	if err != nil {
   840→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   841→	}
   842→
   843→	// Get the branch reference
   844→	ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName), true)
   845→	if err != nil {
   846→		// Try as a remote-style ref
   847→		ref, err = repo.Reference(plumbing.ReferenceName("refs/heads/"+branchName), true)
   848→		if err != nil {
   849→			return "", false
   850→		}
   851→	}
   852→
   853→	// Get the commit
   854→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   855→	if err != nil {
   856→		return "", false
   857→	}
   858→
   859→	// Get the tree
   860→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   861→	if err != nil {
   862→		return "", false
   863→	}
   864→
   865→	// Get the file
   866→	file, err := tree.File(filePath)
   867→	if err != nil {
   868→		return "", false
   869→	}
   870→
   871→	// Get the content
   872→	content, err := file.Contents()
   873→	if err != nil {
   874→		return "", false
   875→	}
   876→
   877→	return content, true
   878→}

   880→// GetLatestCommitMessageOnBranch returns the commit message of the latest commit on the given branch.
   881→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCommitMessageOnBranch(branchName string) string {
   882→	env.T.Helper()
   883→
   884→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   885→	if err != nil {
   886→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   887→	}
   888→
   889→	// Get the branch reference
   890→	ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName), true)
   891→	if err != nil {
   892→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get branch %s reference: %v", branchName, err)
   893→	}
   894→
   895→	// Get the commit
   896→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   897→	if err != nil {
   898→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get commit object: %v", err)
   899→	}
   900→
   901→	return commit.Message
   902→}
   903→
   904→// GitCommitWithShadowHooks stages and commits files, simulating the prepare-commit-msg
   905→// and post-commit hooks as a human (with TTY). This is the default for tests.
   906→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithShadowHooks(message string, files ...string) {
   907→	env.T.Helper()
   908→	env.gitCommitWithShadowHooks(message, true, files...)
   909→}
   910→
   911→// GitCommitWithShadowHooksAsAgent is like GitCommitWithShadowHooks but simulates
   912→// an agent commit (no TTY). This triggers the fast path in PrepareCommitMsg that
   913→// skips content detection and interactive prompts for ACTIVE sessions.
   914→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithShadowHooksAsAgent(message string, files ...string) {
   915→	env.T.Helper()
   916→	env.gitCommitWithShadowHooks(message, false, files...)
   917→}
   918→
   919→// gitCommitWithShadowHooks is the shared implementation for committing with shadow hooks.
   920→// When simulateTTY is true, sets ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 to simulate a human at the terminal.
   921→// When false, filters it out to simulate an agent subprocess (no controlling terminal).
   922→func (env *TestEnv) gitCommitWithShadowHooks(message string, simulateTTY bool, files ...string) {
   923→	env.T.Helper()
   924→
   925→	// Stage files using go-git
   926→	for _, file := range files {
   927→		env.GitAdd(file)
   928→	}
   929→
   930→	// Create a temp file for the commit message (prepare-commit-msg hook modifies this)
   931→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
   932→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
   933→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
   934→	}
   935→
   936→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook using the shared binary.
   937→	// Pass source="message" to match real `git commit -m` behavior.
   938→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile, "message")

   992→// GitCommitAmendWithShadowHooks amends the last commit with shadow hooks.
   993→// This simulates `git commit --amend` with the prepare-commit-msg and post-commit hooks.
   994→// The prepare-commit-msg hook is called with "commit" source to indicate an amend.

  1055→// GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved stages and commits files, simulating what happens when
  1056→// a user removes the Entire-Checkpoint trailer during commit message editing.
  1057→// This tests the opt-out behavior where removing the trailer skips condensation.
  1058→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved(message string, files ...string) {
  1059→	env.T.Helper()
  1060→
  1061→	// Stage files using go-git
  1062→	for _, file := range files {
  1063→		env.GitAdd(file)
  1064→	}
  1065→
  1066→	// Create a temp file for the commit message (prepare-commit-msg hook modifies this)
  1067→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
  1068→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
  1069→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
  1070→	}
  1071→
  1072→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook using the shared binary.
  1073→	// Set ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 to simulate human (this tests the editor flow where
  1074→	// the user removes the trailer before committing).
  1075→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile)
  1076→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1077→	prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1")
  1078→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1079→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg output: %s", output)
  1080→	}
  1081→
  1082→	// Read the modified message (with trailer added by hook)
  1083→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
  1084→	if err != nil {
  1085→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
  1086→	}
  1087→
  1088→	// REMOVE the Entire-Checkpoint trailer (simulating user editing the message)
  1089→	lines := strings.Split(string(modifiedMsg), "\n")
  1090→	var cleanedLines []string
  1091→	for _, line := range lines {
  1092→		// Skip the trailer and the comments about it
  1093→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:") {
  1094→			continue
  1095→		}
  1096→		if strings.Contains(line, "Remove the Entire-Checkpoint trailer") {
  1097→			continue
  1098→		}
  1099→		if strings.Contains(line, "trailer will be added to your next commit") {
  1100→			continue
  1101→		}
  1102→		cleanedLines = append(cleanedLines, line)
  1103→	}
  1104→	cleanedMsg := strings.TrimRight(strings.Join(cleanedLines, "\n"), "\n") + "\n"
  1105→
  1106→	// Create the commit using go-git with the cleaned message (no trailer)
  1107→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1108→	if err != nil {
  1109→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1110→	}
  1111→
  1112→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
  1113→	if err != nil {
  1114→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
  1115→	}
  1116→
  1117→	_, err = worktree.Commit(cleanedMsg, &git.CommitOptions{
  1118→		Author: &object.Signature{
  1119→			Name:  "Test User",
  1120→			Email: "test@example.com",
  1121→			When:  time.Now(),
  1122→		},
  1123→	})
  1124→	if err != nil {
  1125→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
  1126→	}
  1127→
  1128→	// Run post-commit hook - since trailer was removed, no condensation should happen
  1129→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
  1130→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1131→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1132→		env.T.Logf("post-commit output: %s", output)
  1133→	}
  1134→}

  1136→// ListBranchesWithPrefix returns all branches that start with the given prefix.
  1137→func (env *TestEnv) ListBranchesWithPrefix(prefix string) []string {
  1138→	env.T.Helper()
  1139→
  1140→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1141→	if err != nil {
  1142→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1143→	}
  1144→
  1145→	refs, err := repo.References()
  1146→	if err != nil {
  1147→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get references: %v", err)
  1148→	}
  1149→
  1150→	var branches []string
  1151→	_ = refs.ForEach(func(ref *plumbing.Reference) error {
  1152→		name := ref.Name().Short()
  1153→		if len(name) >= len(prefix) && name[:len(prefix)] == prefix {
  1154→			branches = append(branches, name)
  1155→		}
  1156→		return nil
  1157→	})
  1158→
  1159→	return branches
  1160→}

  1162→// GetLatestCheckpointID returns the most recent checkpoint ID from the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch.
  1163→// This is used by tests that previously extracted the checkpoint ID from commit message trailers.
  1164→// Now that active branch commits are clean (no trailers), we get the ID from the sessions branch.
  1165→// Fatals if the checkpoint ID cannot be found, with detailed context about what was found.
  1166→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCheckpointID() string {
  1167→	env.T.Helper()
  1168→
  1169→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1170→	if err != nil {
  1171→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1172→	}
  1173→
  1174→	// Get the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch
  1175→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName)
  1176→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
  1177→	if err != nil {
  1178→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get %s branch: %v", paths.MetadataBranchName, err)
  1179→	}
  1180→
  1181→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
  1182→	if err != nil {
  1183→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get commit: %v", err)
  1184→	}
  1185→
  1186→	// Extract checkpoint ID from commit message
  1187→	// Format: "Checkpoint: <12-hex-char-id>\n\nSession: ...\nStrategy: ..."
  1188→	for _, line := range strings.Split(commit.Message, "\n") {
  1189→		line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
  1190→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Checkpoint: ") {
  1191→			return strings.TrimPrefix(line, "Checkpoint: ")
  1192→		}
  1193→	}
  1194→
  1195→	env.T.Fatalf("could not find checkpoint ID in %s branch commit message:\n%s",
  1196→		paths.MetadataBranchName, commit.Message)
  1197→	return ""
  1198→}

  1240→// GetCheckpointIDFromCommitMessage extracts the Entire-Checkpoint trailer from a commit message.
  1241→// Returns empty string if no trailer found.
  1242→func (env *TestEnv) GetCheckpointIDFromCommitMessage(commitSHA string) string {
  1243→	env.T.Helper()
  1244→
  1245→	msg := env.GetCommitMessage(commitSHA)
  1246→	cpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(msg)
  1247→	if !found {
  1248→		return ""
  1249→	}
  1250→	return cpID.String()
  1251→}
  1252→
  1253→// GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory walks backwards from HEAD on the active branch
  1254→// and returns the checkpoint ID from the first commit that has an Entire-Checkpoint trailer.
  1255→// This verifies that condensation actually happened (commit has trailer) without relying
  1256→// on timestamp-based matching.
  1257→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory() string {
  1258→	env.T.Helper()
  1259→
  1260→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1261→	if err != nil {
  1262→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1263→	}
  1264→
  1265→	head, err := repo.Head()
  1266→	if err != nil {
  1267→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
  1268→	}
  1269→
  1270→	commitIter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: head.Hash()})
  1271→	if err != nil {
  1272→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to iterate commits: %v", err)
  1273→	}
  1274→
  1275→	var checkpointID string
  1276→	//nolint:errcheck // ForEach callback handles errors
  1277→	commitIter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error {
  1278→		if cpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(c.Message); found {
  1279→			checkpointID = cpID.String()
  1280→			return errors.New("stop iteration") // Found it, stop
  1281→		}
  1282→		return nil
  1283→	})
  1284→
  1285→	if checkpointID == "" {
  1286→		env.T.Fatalf("no commit with Entire-Checkpoint trailer found in history")
  1287→	}
  1288→
  1289→	return checkpointID
  1290→}

  1295→func ShardedCheckpointPath(checkpointID string) string {

  1299→// SessionFilePath returns the path to a session file within a checkpoint.
  1300→// Session files are stored in numbered subdirectories using 0-based indexing (e.g., 0/full.jsonl).
  1301→// This function constructs the path for the first (default) session.
  1302→func SessionFilePath(checkpointID string, fileName string) string {
  1303→	return id.CheckpointID(checkpointID).Path() + "/0/" + fileName
  1304→}

  1306→// CheckpointSummaryPath returns the path to the root metadata.json (CheckpointSummary) for a checkpoint.
  1307→func CheckpointSummaryPath(checkpointID string) string {
  1308→	return id.CheckpointID(checkpointID).Path() + "/" + paths.MetadataFileName
  1309→}

  1311→// SessionMetadataPath returns the path to the session-level metadata.json for a checkpoint.
  1312→func SessionMetadataPath(checkpointID string) string {

  1314→}

  1316→func findModuleRoot() string {
  1317→	// Start from this source file's location and walk up to find go.mod
  1318→	_, thisFile, _, ok := runtime.Caller(0)
  1319→	if !ok {
  1320→		panic("failed to get current file path via runtime.Caller")
  1321→	}
  1322→	dir := filepath.Dir(thisFile)
  1323→
  1324→	for {
  1325→		if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(dir, "go.mod")); err == nil {
  1326→			return dir
  1327→		}
  1328→		parent := filepath.Dir(dir)
  1329→		if parent == dir {
  1330→			panic("could not find go.mod starting from " + thisFile)
  1331→		}
  1332→		dir = parent
  1333→	}
  1334→}

    59→// SimulateUserPromptSubmitWithResponse simulates the UserPromptSubmit hook
    60→// and returns the parsed hook response (for testing blocking behavior).
    61→func (r *HookRunner) SimulateUserPromptSubmitWithResponse(sessionID string) (*HookResponse, error) {
    62→	r.T.Helper()

    76→	// If hook failed with an error, return the error

    82→	// Parse JSON response from stdout

    84→	if len(output.Stdout) > 0 {

    86→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse hook response: %w\nStdout: %s",
    87→				err, output.Stdout)
    88→		}
    89→	}
    90→
    91→	return &resp, nil

   130→	hookInput := map[string]interface{}{
   131→		"session_id":      input.SessionID,
   132→		"transcript_path": input.TranscriptPath,
   133→		"tool_use_id":     input.ToolUseID,
   134→		"tool_input": map[string]string{
   135→			"subagent_type": input.SubagentType,
   136→			"description":   input.Description,
   137→		},
   138→	}
   139→
   140→	return r.runHookWithInput("pre-task", hookInput)
   141→}
   142→
   143→// PostTaskInput contains the input for PostToolUse[Task] hook.

   168→func (r *HookRunner) runHookWithInput(flag string, input interface{}) error {
   169→	r.T.Helper()
   170→
   171→	inputJSON, err := json.Marshal(input)
   172→	if err != nil {
   173→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal hook input: %w", err)
   174→	}
   175→
   176→	return r.runHookInRepoDir(flag, inputJSON)
   177→}
   178→
   179→func (r *HookRunner) runHookInRepoDir(hookName string, inputJSON []byte) error {
   180→	// Run using the shared test binary
   181→	// Command structure: entire hooks claude-code <hook-name>
   182→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "claude-code", hookName)
   183→	cmd.Dir = r.RepoDir
   184→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
   185→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   186→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+r.ClaudeProjectDir,
   187→	)
   188→
   189→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   190→	if err != nil {
   191→		return fmt.Errorf("hook %s failed: %w\nInput: %s\nOutput: %s",
   192→			hookName, err, inputJSON, output)
   193→	}
   194→
   195→	r.T.Logf("Hook %s output: %s", hookName, output)
   196→	return nil
   197→}

   199→// Session represents a simulated Claude Code session.
   200→type Session struct {
   201→	ID                string // Raw model session ID (e.g., "test-session-1")
   202→	TranscriptPath    string
   203→	TranscriptBuilder *TranscriptBuilder
   204→	env               *TestEnv
   205→}
   206→
   207→// FileChange represents a file modification in a session.
   208→type FileChange struct {
   209→	Path    string
   210→	Content string
   211→}
   212→
   213→// NewSession creates a new simulated session.
   214→func (env *TestEnv) NewSession() *Session {
   215→	env.T.Helper()
   216→
   217→	env.SessionCounter++
   218→	sessionID := fmt.Sprintf("test-session-%d", env.SessionCounter)
   219→
   220→	transcriptPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".entire", "tmp", sessionID+".jsonl")
   221→
   222→	return &Session{
   223→		ID:                sessionID,
   224→		TranscriptPath:    transcriptPath,
   225→		TranscriptBuilder: NewTranscriptBuilder(),
   226→		env:               env,
   227→	}
   228→}
   229→
   230→// CreateTranscript builds and writes a transcript for the session.
   231→func (s *Session) CreateTranscript(prompt string, changes []FileChange) string {
   232→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddUserMessage(prompt)
   233→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddAssistantMessage("I'll help you with that.")
   234→
   235→	for _, change := range changes {
   236→		toolID := s.TranscriptBuilder.AddToolUse("mcp__acp__Write", change.Path, change.Content)
   237→		s.TranscriptBuilder.AddToolResult(toolID)
   238→	}
   239→
   240→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddAssistantMessage("Done!")
   241→
   242→	if err := s.TranscriptBuilder.WriteToFile(s.TranscriptPath); err != nil {
   243→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to write transcript: %v", err)
   244→	}
   245→
   246→	return s.TranscriptPath
   247→}
